in writing. − i support the report on the proposal for a regulation on the exercise of the union's rights to implement and enforce international trade law, as the eu currently lacks a horizontal legislative framework to enforce its rights under the wto and other international trade agreements. the proposed regulation aims to enable the union to establish a comprehensive and effective framework to allow immediate action to be taken for the effective exercise of the union's rights under international trade agreements in order to protect its economic interests. the main ideas in this report are mainly aimed at providing a structured and systematic exchange of information between the commission and parliament without delay in the decision-making process.
in writing. − i consider the report on the amended proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council on the european maritime and fisheries fund to be clear and appropriate. the changes are good and include several key aspects that directly affect bulgaria. i voted in favour of guaranteeing a level playing field for producer organisations throughout the european union and of making direct funding available for the development of production and marketing plans. i also welcome the proposals for sustainable and cohesive territorial development of areas dependent on fishing. i agree that the emff should support the development, competitiveness and sustainability of fisheries.
in writing. − following the presentation in june of the interim report of the ep's special committee on combating organised crime, corruption and money laundering (crim), we voted today on the final version of the document. all of us who supported the report are united in our understanding that the serious challenges posed by crossborder crime require a pan-european response. the commission has also made a number of recommendations to member states and the commission on the steps to be taken in the period 2014-2019. the objectives include measures to prevent trafficking in human beings and cybercrime and the introduction of best practices related to the confiscation of property acquired through criminal activity. in addition to recommendations to promote administrative, judicial and police cooperation at european and international level, the report explicitly emphasises the role of investigative journalism in uncovering cases of fraud, corruption and organised crime. i cannot help but emphasise the call for the criminalisation of vote buying during elections, as well as the measures relating to stricter controls on the financing and accountability of political parties. i am convinced that by improving the regulatory framework and enhancing cooperation between member states, as well as by introducing measures at european level, the fight against these negative phenomena will be more effective.
in writing. - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i supported the report because there is hardly anyone who doubts the urgent need to fight effectively against organised crime, corruption and money laundering. these phenomena have acquired global dimensions and require an adequate response, with appropriate legal instruments to be established at community level. along with violence and intimidation, corruption is a typical method of operation of organized crime. money laundering is closely linked to corruption, tax evasion, and tax evasion. although by their very nature they are separate phenomena, the existence of such interconnections requires common targeted actions. the report of the temporary committee proposes a single and coherent legislative framework to strike at the economic heart of organised crime, which operates across national borders and territories, and to strengthen judicial and police cooperation at european and international level. at the same time, i welcome a more responsible policy on the vulnerability of public administrations to corruption, a policy on swifter and more reliable criminal justice, a more stable entrepreneurship, a more transparent banking and professional system, and appropriate measures against money laundering to prevent crime from continuing to be a profitable activity that harms the legitimate economy, honest entrepreneurs and citizens.
in writing. - conscious of the economic motivation and without neglecting the environmental benefits, i voted against the gaston franco report. the reasons for this are that i think it is unreasonable in a country like bulgaria during an economic crisis to make decisions on such large-scale investments that do not meet the standards. in a member state where the need for a comprehensive change in established patterns of governance, behaviour, business, consumption, etc. is paramount, concrete measures are needed for the urgent agenda, and a similar programme, which consumes a huge resource, would rather cause harm. in addition, i would like to note that if environmental commitments are undertaken at european and international level, the likely lack of political will and the difference in implementation between member states could lead to additional administrative burdens.
in writing. − it is my personal view that the revision of the council directive laying down basic safety standards is the next necessary step to improve protection against exposure to ionising radiation. the text of the directive laying down basic safety standards for protection against the dangers arising from exposure to ionising radiation has been optimised in a meaningful way by including necessary additions, such as the recommendation to distinguish between existing exposure situations linked to natural radioactivity and existing exposure situations due to human activity, additions on a thorough study of the impact that ionising radiation has on both people and the environment, the effective management of nuclear accidents with cross-border consequences, etc.
in writing. - i welcome the brok report on the common foreign and security policy. this is a very important area for the eu in its development. the report also pays particular attention to the eu's relations with its strategic partners in the field of peace, security and socioeconomic development. as regards eu cooperation with the us, the report stresses that to build trust, the us needs to respect sensitive data protection legislation and change its data collection activities targeting the eu and its citizens. this is a very important point at a time like this, when serious problems and doubts are ripe.
in writing. i voted for this report because it is a rich report and it emphasises the priorities of the eu's foreign and security policy. the report highlights in particular the need for coordination between the various actors in eu foreign policy in third countries (special representatives, heads of delegation and heads of mission). this should give the eu greater visibility on the international stage. i would add that one of the eu's strategic partnerships is with africa, working with regional (au) and sub-regional organisations such as ecowas and eecsc. the cfsp must also focus on preventive diplomacy in a comprehensive approach, with the eu acting as mediator. i also voted in favour of this report because we must support the efforts of the high representative to secure a permanent seat on the un security council. this would be a great step forward for the cfsp. finally, i welcome the momentum created by catherine ashton for the inclusion of more women in eu foreign policy.
in writing. - i support the report. the security system the eu is seeking to build for itself is ambitious and unique. on the one hand, we are still building the european external action service, on the other hand, in the eu we are looking at foreign affairs, security and defence policy in an integrated way, parallel to nato's priorities for smart defence. from this perspective, i believe we are on the right track, and i want to congratulate lady catherine ashton and all those involved. as bulgaria is neighbour to some of the countries covered in mr brock's report and is affected by the situation in syria, i welcome the eeas' impressive work on kosovo-serbia relations; i am pleased to see a focus on the strategic partnership with turkey, as well as a reaffirmation of enlargement as a key eu instrument. but - and i share the view of mrs neynski - in the development of the particular way in which the eu conducts its foreign policy, budget cuts bring a particular uncertainty to our future actions. 'efficiency' should therefore be the keyword for the coming years, which also means increased coordination with the european parliament from the outset. if we succeed in this endeavor, we will see a united and active, rather than reactive, foreign policy.
in writing. − i supported this report because, on the one hand, i believe that the development of telecommunications in the eu is one of the main drivers of economic growth in the union, and on the other hand, because the report criticises the european commission for not yet having made a proper review of the telecommunications framework directive. this framework was adopted in 2007 and its only revision was in 2009. four years have passed since then and it is high time that the regulatory framework was revised again in order to identify problems and identify areas for improvement.
mr president, i take the opportunity to see commissioner vassiliou here because i want to introduce her to a problem which i think we can solve together here as european politicians. as a former athlete, i support the inclusion of sport in the erasmus programme. funding motivates young people to grow, but i know money isn't the main motivator. motivation comes when your efforts pay off in tournaments. whatever funding is there, if the young people don't show up, it's all gone. european artistic gymnastics is discriminated against for the youth olympic games, the work of the best european artistic gymnastics schools is destroyed. there will be six ensembles, with the continent hosting two. i believe there's been a technical error. i dare not think that it was done deliberately, avoiding the participation of some european countries. no country of the european union will participate in this allocation. countries on two continents or even outside europe, such as russia, kazakhstan, turkey and others, will benefit. we must do everything to correct this injustice by demanding that the number of ensembles in europe be three. i call for immediate action because we must show that we can protect the rights of european citizens.
mr president,the programming of 'erasmus for all' is the expected step towards improving cooperation mechanisms in the field of education and now also in sport, in order to achieve the objectives of the europe 2020 programme. this really shows that the european union is putting education first. i'll make three points: first, the inclusion of support for innovation, which will greatly improve the motivation of the young generation and the academic staff. secondly, the programme will be much more successful if certain specific procedures in its implementation are reconsidered, for example the harmonisation of the funding conditions for participation in the different activities. the difference in rates for both students and teachers puts some countries at a disadvantage and makes them an unattractive destination. the differences in the pay of participants create a precondition for inequality and by regulating the geography of the programme, we should reciprocally regulate the financial parameters in order to achieve good partnerships. thirdly, i welcome the simplification of procedures, and i hope that some of them will be revised to make the programme more successful.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, as others have said before me, there are in fact no meps who know better than we, who are elected from eastern europe and have to travel several thousand kilometres each week from our countries to the european parliament's offices, how important the development of european transport networks is. so i am of course in favour of this initiative and this european project. we now understand that the new programming period is preparing for a greater development of rail transport, of railway infrastructure, but here i want to draw attention to something that in addition to the infrastructure itself, i think that investments should also be made, especially in the railways, in terms of what moves on this infrastructure. five years ago in bulgaria, a train, because of the old cars, because of the old composition, caught fire and burned and several dozen people died. however, a year and a half ago, the european commission refused to allow bulgaria to make what i would call vital, life-saving investments in its railways, to develop them and bring them up to a modern european level so that people would want to use them. because the bulgarian railways are collapsing right now, precisely because nobody wants to ride in murder cars. and that is why, commissioner, when you give with one hand and take with the other, it does not leave a good impression on european citizens. they mostly see the latter.
ladies and gentlemen, i know you've been following what's been happening in bulgaria in recent months, but i'm here today to tell you that there are actually two bulgarians - one of the obscure political and economic interests behind the oresharski cabinet, which has taken thousands of people out on the streets of bulgaria in recent months in an attempt to confront the mafia and corruption. in this bulgaria, the legal order is giving way to administrative arbitrariness, solidarity is giving way to racism and xenophobia, institutions are subordinated to corporate interests. but today i am here to ask you to support another bulgaria - the one that has been protesting for more than 150 days for morality and values, the bulgaria of bright and uncompromising minds, of people with the courage to defend principles in a time without principles, of those who are not afraid to say out loud that there are other things besides bread that are worth protesting for, a young bulgaria that is determined not to live in compromise like its parents. this bulgaria is not afraid of the shields of heavily armed police, of midnight inspections and interrogations. she wants change, not replacement. for this bulgaria, i ask your support here, in the heart of europe, as 15 years ago they supported our aspiration.
mr president, i would like to address the president of the european parliament, mr schulz, and mr freedom. i'm sure that as true european social democrats, they understand the difficult situation they have found themselves in with the election of their chairman sergei stanishev, who comes from the unreformed bulgarian communist party. the current bulgarian government came to power after heavy manipulation in the pre-election period and is supported by dubious oligarchic circles and outright anti-european forces. the scandalous enth-business-national-socialist government of bulgaria, which serves moscow's geopolitical interests, is a disgrace to european social democracy. the sooner you get rid of this burden, dear european social democrats, the better it is for your european election campaign and reputation. just two examples: the prime minister announced that he would fire civil servants who protest against the government and bulgaria would almost triple the budget for wiretaps, which is absolutely unjustified, together with the changes in the "security" sector, which led to the collapse of confidence in the national assembly to 11%. i hope for the necessary reaction from the european social democrats for a democratic solution to the political crisis in bulgaria.
the rapporteur. - mr president, i am glad that we have reached this day and this moment when we are discussing for the last time and i hope that today we will adopt the multiannual financial framework. this has been a very long journey for parliament. we started three years ago with a special sure committee, in which we presented, before the commission's proposal, parliament's vision for the next seven years, after which we had very long, very difficult negotiations with the council. i am glad that they are over and i am pleased that europe will have its own budget from next year for the next seven years. we could have done more. and by we could, i mean all the institutions of the european union. we could have made the european budget a much more compelling growth instrument than it is today. we could have made the european budget help the countries in difficulty, the regions, and show that there is added value in it. we could, and the member states could, do this, to understand that every euro invested in the european budget is more efficient than the euro invested in the national investment budget, and that the european budget does what national budgets cannot do in a time of crisis - public investment - what our economies need. we couldn't do that. on the contrary, i think we've actually achieved results that are good and that provide a good foundation. first, despite the unprecedented reduction, never before in the history of the european union, of the union budget, we have agreed on a mechanism that will make the use of funds much more efficient and what is promised will be spent and paid for european projects. secondly, and i am glad that the socialist group has been extremely active in this respect, we have a new instrument to combat youth unemployment and the youth guarantee - with 6 billion euros for the first two years. something that is extremely important, but it's not enough. the problem is, with these tools, we can only signal, not solve the problem. we managed to agree on 400 million euros to be spent earlier to help the european economy get out of the crisis - for youth projects, for science and research, for the competitiveness of the economy. we have defended the fund for the poorest of our fellow citizens who need help - the fund for the poorest of europe's citizens. these are accomplishments that are real. we're very much about mid-term review. we are holding because i am convinced that in two years' time it will be clear that the european budget can be used much better, much more successfully and it can produce much better results. we will insist on continuing the talks on our own resources and on achieving a result on our own resources. the european budget is currently seen by national parliaments as a burden. it is not normal, it is not a burden, it helps the member states and therefore it should be sufficiently autonomous, not with new taxes, but with much more autonomy and guaranteeing its own resources. and finally, within this budget framework, we have agreed on a number of policies. one of them, which raises a lot of objections, including in my group of socialists and democrats, is the policy on macroeconomic conditions. it is not normal to punish countries and regions that have economic problems additionally, and i am convinced that this measure is not good. yes, we have improved it compared to the original proposal, but i am convinced that this is an area where we need to work further so that the european budget can help rather than hinder the national countries. with this result, i am confident that today we will be able to support the multiannual financial framework and parliament will be able to fulfil its commitment to the council.
president-in-office of the council. − mr president, honourable members, this morning’s debate on the multiannual financial framework (mff) is the culmination of a long process. first of all, after an enormous amount of work by all our institutions, we reached an agreement in june. i would like to thank warmly all those who were involved in the mff negotiation process. i know that, from the very start, this house, and president schulz personally, played a major role in securing a final deal. then we entered yet another round of codecision process on 70 related legislative proposals. we have rarely had tougher negotiations, even in the crisis years, but in the end we are on the right track for reaching agreement on all the proposals. so we can now look forward to releasing the funds that will provide a major boost to our economies in areas such as research, innovation, education, culture, cohesion and agriculture. this is an investment budget: a budget our economies need to grow. it is a budget that supports our policies in the area of justice and home affairs and a budget that strengthens the eu’s external action. in short, the multiannual financial framework is what it takes to translate great ideas into tangible action both within the union and globally. this multiannual financial framework is clearly a compromise solution, but it is also a solution that creates opportunities for our people. therefore, i consider it a success which we have achieved together. i turn now to you, honourable members, and i confirm that the european parliament played an unprecedented role in the negotiations. not only have you defended your positions with strength and conviction but you have also succeeded in making the framework more flexible and you have managed to convince others of the need for a review in the future. you have injected more transparency into the eu budget and you also ensured that the process will continue, since we are already preparing to set up a high-level group on own resources. today we can see the bigger picture, which consists not only of the mff itself – i.e. the regulation on the interinstitutional agreement and the declarations – but also of the draft amending budgets, the sectoral legislation and the preparatory work on the high-level group on own resources. the lithuanian presidency is fully committed to making this group operational before the end of this year. moreover, as we confirmed in our letter to mr lamassoure, we are ready to organise an inaugural meeting of the high-level group on 18 or 19 december at the latest. the vote on the mff is eagerly awaited by many, and first and foremost by our citizens. they will be able to use new roads, new infrastructure and new telecoms networks; they will reap the benefits of research, participate in erasmus exchanges and benefit from european funding, in one way or another, in their everyday lives. i would like to conclude by thanking all those who, on behalf of your institution, contributed to making it happen: to president schulz and the whole parliament negotiating team, headed by alain lamassoure. my thanks also go to president barroso and commissioner lewandowski and to president van rompuy and our heads of state and government, who paved the way for an agreement at their february meeting, and of course to my various predecessors in the council presidency, all of whom have put a huge amount of work and energy into this matter since the commission brought forward its proposal on the mff more than two years ago. it has taken the joint efforts of all of us to make this agreement possible. we look forward now to a positive vote later this morning which will enable us to submit the mff package for adoption by the council.
member of the commission. − mr president, todayʼs vote – and hopefully agreement – in this house will be, i am sure, one of the milestones of this parliamentʼs mandate because this is about the huge work on legislation and budgetary decisions. this is the final act in long and complex negotiations, not long and complex because this – i mean the multiannual budgets enshrined in the treaties – is happening for the first time but because of the exceptional context of europe in crisis. for the commission and myself this has really been a very long story. it started as early as 2009 with a budgetary review. this was followed by the draft in june 2011: still, i believe, responding better to the needs of europe, being built originally on the principle of financing more of europe with the same money. then came two rounds of intergovernmental wrangling, with herman van rompuy and josé manuel barroso doing their best to reconcile the positions of the member states, which were further apart than ever because of these exceptional circumstances. finally, parliament joined the action and i can understand the house’s frustration. it was not that easy under the treaty conditions to react to the unanimous decision of the member states and a deal made between 28 member states. therefore, i really acknowledge the intelligent, pragmatic and finally successful approach of parliament in the way it worked to improve the architecture of the financial framework for seven-year periods. it was not just about the mff, but also about the most pressing needs of 2013 and about the high-level group on own resources, to be set up this year in order to reflect on better, more transparent ways of financing the european budget in future. therefore, once more i acknowledge that intelligent, successful approach, the personal involvement of president schulz, and the role played by the committee on budgets and alain lamassoure and the negotiating team. i think this is a crowning moment also for the lithuanian presidency. once again, i acknowledge the personal involvement of minister rimkūnas in the final hours of our negotiations on all the issues – 2013, the mff and the budget for 2014, the first budgetary year of the new financial perspective – at the meeting in november, in a conciliation that was not that easy. our role was that of facilitator, providing solutions and overcoming bottlenecks. therefore, the role of hervé jouanjean and my creative chef de cabinet, marc lemaître, also needs to be acknowledged. indeed, a long list of personalities have paved the way towards todayʼs agreement. as to the shape and architecture of this financial perspective, we do perhaps need more for a europe with enlarged competences, but parliament in its priorities was absolutely right in finding answers to the lower volumes. we have eur 40 billion less for the future than we have now. this has never happened before in the history of the european union. the right answer is flexibility, and this goes beyond special instruments such as flexibility instruments, as it is about global margins and payments and commitments. it was due to the efforts by parliament that we have that unusual flexibility, which is the right answer to the lower volumes for the future. there are also clear novelties in the budget, and therefore there is decent modernisation of the budget. by this i mean the connecting europe facility, which is about a europe that is still badly connected, and the youth employment initiative as means of mass access to finance via the blending of various resources including the capacities of the european investment bank. despite the lower volumes, we have reinforced erasmus and reinforced research and development and some external policy instruments. above all, the multiannual framework is about predictability, and about stability of investment over seven years. this is a value in itself amidst the uncertainty of the europe of today. this is a real value delivered to europe. above all, our agreement – hopefully to be adopted today – is evidence that the european institutions are working, and that the consensus machine of the european union is functioning and delivering. i therefore think this is a good day for europe.
the rapporteur on the opinion of the committee on regional development. - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, europe 2020 has set us very ambitious and beautiful goals, many of them copied from the lisbon strategy, which we failed to achieve in 2010. will we be able to achieve these goals in 2020 with the proposed financial framework, or will we rewrite them for 2030? unfortunately, the budget was shaped by the mentality of the crisis in europe and by populists who took advantage of it to achieve their own political goals, but it is still better to have a reduced budget than no budget at all. the european budget is an investment instrument and as such must be able to be used in the best possible way in the european regions. national and local authorities in the member states are called upon to ensure this effective uptake. by 2020, all countries in the european union, and especially the backward ones, which unfortunately include my country bulgaria, must reach the current average living standard in europe. this budget is a breath of fresh air for local government, especially for the poorest regions, such as the north-west in the bulgarian regions. i am glad that certain conditions which parliament has insisted on have been accepted: mid-term review, flexibility in spending, own resources and unity and transparency of the budget. this will ensure a budget that is functional, consistent and more responsive to the needs of citizens and businesses in the european union.
rapporteur. − mr president, i know that the budget is a very good topic for discussing and defending the political positions of the groups. it is also a very good moment to take responsibilities. all the colleagues who claim – and i would support very much what the commissioner said – that there is no result from the negotiations between parliament and the council have simply missed the last nine months in parliament. we have achieved many more improvements and structural changes to the european budget. yes, it is true that we are not happy with the size of it. however, i am sure that this is the time to start working and looking ahead and i would like to inform the commission and the council of this. the margins are not enough and the council has to be prepared to react outside the budget, with additional resources, to new circumstances that may appear. these own resources are an absolute must – not only the establishment of the working group but also the result in 2016 – and parliament is going to insist on this. the payments have to be secured and we are going to keep insisting on that. the council and the commission have to make the european added value of the budget much more visible and we are going to make sure that at the mid-term review, the value of the european budget is much more visible than it is now. finally, i would like to thank all my colleagues that i had the privilege to work with: alain lamassoure, jean-luc dehaene, reimer böge, anne e. jensen, the commission and several councils where we reached this agreement and, of course, i would thank my group very much for the support i have had during all that time.
on behalf of the s&d group. – mr president, i speak on behalf of our shadow rapporteur, eider gardiazábal, who is on the way to strasbourg right now. of course, we cannot be happy with the size of the budget, and i think we are going to have this conversation over the next seven years given the financial framework that we have agreed. but i think that, within this constraint, the positive fact is that parliament was able to restore commitment levels to the level proposed in the draft budget by the commission, and we did not allow a decrease. we have a slight increase in some important areas such as the digital agenda, the social dialogue, micro-finance and issues that help the recovery of the economy, as well as humanitarian aid and the palestine allocations, which are important to us. the big problem, though, in next year’s budget is in the level of payments. despite the fact that we have succeeded in increasing by eur 500 million the initial council position, we all know that the payments are not going to be enough next year. so when we insist on reflecting this, using the flexibility instrument or the contingency margin, we are absolutely sure that next year we will have to have recourse to it. as the presidency mentioned, we are going to stick very much to the declaration which has been adopted by the two institutions. the european union has to pay all the bills that are put on the table. that means that next year the council has to be ready to have recourse to all the instruments of the mff, including the contingency and the flexibility instruments, if such need arises, and this is going to be very important for parliament.
in writing. − i give my consent to the draft council regulation laying down the multiannual financial framework for the years 2014-2020. i believe that the report covers all aspects, that the budgetary adjustments are justified and that the need for a sufficient degree of predictability for the preparation and implementation of medium-term investments over the duration of the mff is taken into account. following the european parliament elections in 2016, a review will be carried out, which will allow the institutions, including the european parliament elected in 2014, to reassess priorities.
in writing. - i voted against this report not because i am against the interinstitutional agreement between the ep, the council and the commission on budgetary discipline, but because i believe that there is a need to increase transparency and to ensure a democratic procedure that allows and guarantees the presence of representatives of all the political groups of the parliament at all stages of the negotiations. i hope that the committee on budgets, in cooperation with the committee on constitutional affairs, will draw the necessary conclusions and proposals before the post-electoral review in 2016 on the terms and conditions for such negotiations, so as to ensure the democratic and transparent nature of the whole budgetary process.
in writing. - i welcome the scholz report, as it takes account of the benefits of the amendment: the public procurement agreement (ppa) seeks, through several explicit provisions, to meet the objectives of establishing a fair level playing field, while providing sufficient scope for policy decisions and diversity, environmental protection, etc. in addition, i would like to note that the revision of the ttber improves its core text, which sets out the rules for conducting tenders by ensuring a higher level of clarity and transparency. the amendment also includes an extended scope a package of additions to market access.
in writing. - i fully support mrs. park's proposal. as a representative of a member state with an outstanding contribution in the field of sport, i consider education and training to be key elements of the europe 2020 strategy for smart, sustainable and inclusive growth, as well as of the integrated guidelines for the economic and employment policies of the member states, and with a view to improving skills and tackling high levels of youth unemployment in many member states.
in writing. i voted in favour of my colleague doris pack's report because it is a very good report, which will achieve both the objectives of streamlining programmes and more transparency, while showing the eu's strong commitment to youth and mobility. the report, in addition to proposing a clear structure, has improved the financing facilities for young people on the move: fairer grants based on the cost of living and living expenses in the destination countries, and a guarantee instrument for master's loans, without parental or third-party guarantee. these are practical arrangements that encourage young people. the report also has the merit of taking into account the need for better matching between higher education and the professional world with knowledge alliances and sectoral alliances. this is particularly important in the fight against youth unemployment in the future.
in writing. − i voted for the proposed texts because erasmus+ is a successful extension of one of the most popular and effective european union programmes in the field of education, culture and youth mobility. the increased budget of the programme for the period 2014-2020 will ensure that more than 4 million europeans in various levels of education can benefit from the chance to improve their knowledge and language skills in another member state. one of the important achievements of the new programme is the creation of a european facility for granting loans on favourable terms to students wishing to pursue master's degrees in other eu countries. the erasmus programme is one of the symbols of a united europe for young people, and i feel a personal responsibility to help make it a reality through my work. more than 11 000 bulgarians have taken the opportunity to join the programme over the last 15 years, which speaks to its popularity in bulgaria, as well as the need for it to be continued and developed. moreover, the educational opportunities provided, the inclusion in traineeship programmes and the improved chances of future professional realisation remain more than adequate in the face of rising youth unemployment across the european union.
madam president, we talk more about the economy, finance and the restrictions regime, but we often forget that the fundamental values of the european union are first and foremost our cultural identity and one of the union's most important messages: unity in diversity. the integration of the existing cultural programmes into a single creative europe is the right approach to help us meet the europe 2020 objectives. the ongoing social fragmentation and the difficulties faced by cultural industries create risks of accumulating cultural poverty in younger generations. we need to provide certainty for the creative industries in several ways: faster and easier access to the tools of the cultural programme; certainty that cultural skills are essential to europe's vision and to enhancing its competitiveness; support for intercultural dialogue: i would draw attention to the need for stronger support for living culture, for the promotion of cultural and historical heritage, because the added value they create will overcome scepticism, rising nationalist sentiments and the construction of dividing lines between states and peoples.
in writing. − one of the main objectives of each eu member state should be to preserve cultural diversity and national identity - something that is particularly important for us as bulgarians. it was therefore a pleasure for me to support the 'programme europe' report. the general objectives of the programme are to preserve and promote europe's cultural and linguistic diversity and to enhance the competitiveness of the sector. the programme will also contribute to the implementation of the europe 2020 strategy. i note as very important the building of links between the cultural and audiovisual sectors by creating a horizontal direction that will help them to deal with the current global challenges: the fragmentation of the european market generated by linguistic and cultural diversity, the need to adapt to globalisation and the transition to digitalisation, the lack of comparable data and the difficulties of smes in the cultural and creative sectors to access credit, all of which require action at union level.
in writing. − i voted 'abstention' on the report by ms takula, not only because of the legal basis, which does not give parliament the power to amend the text of the legislative proposal, but also because of the intention to increase the budget for this programme, which aims to finance cultural and historical initiatives, support activities related to overcoming discrimination and the development of civil society, the participation of young people, women and minority groups in democratic activity (development of the non-governmental sector), at a time when budgetary constraints are foreseen. as a representative of a member state in an exceptional financial crisis, i find this inappropriate. however, i support the idea that the council should discuss the budget proposed by the commission, and the budget will be discussed when the multiannual financial framework is over.
in writing. i voted against this report with my colleagues to allow the adoption of the programme proposed by the commission, because european citizens need this programme as early as 2014. despite the tight budget and the chosen legal basis, which simply excludes the only institution directly elected by the european citizens that this very programme is aimed at, it is a very good programme, particularly important for the future of the eu. it is about bringing european citizens closer to the reality of the eu, but also bringing european citizens closer to each other, relying on memory as a common foundation for planning for the future through projects that promote citizens' participation in shaping europe. the european institutions must be made accessible, but every citizen must also be encouraged to take up his rights and duties as a citizen. i am also pleased that the council has taken into account two key elements proposed by parliament: intercultural dialogue in the objectives of the programme and geographical balance in its management.
in writing. − the report on the connecting europe facility is clear and reasoned, and i therefore fully support it. in order to achieve smart, sustainable and inclusive growth and to stimulate job creation, the union needs modern and high-performance infrastructure that contributes to the connectivity and integration of the union and all its regions, in particular in the areas of transport, energy and telecommunications. this is a prerequisite for greater economic, social and territorial cohesion, and modernisation will indeed help to enhance the free movement of people, goods, capital and ideas by facilitating cross-border links. the connecting europe facility will contribute to the objective of growth and competitiveness: by providing the public transport engineering sector with the means to implement infrastructure projects; by stimulating research, technology transfer and development (financial and operational) in these sectors; by providing the eu with sound, efficient and reliable transport, energy and telecommunications systems that contribute to the global competitiveness of the eu economy, to the harmonious and integrated functioning of the union by improving economic, social and environmental performance and territorial integration.

in writing. − a number of legislative acts in the field of agricultural and fisheries statistics confer implementing powers on the commission in respect of some of the provisions of these legislative acts. with the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, it is necessary to align those implementing powers with the treaty on the functioning of the european union. i regard the vote on this report as a routine procedure, with the general aim of all legislation to remove all provisions relating to the regulatory procedure with scrutiny by the end of the seventh parliamentary term. the aim is to amend nine legislative acts in the field of agricultural and fisheries statistics in order to bring them into line with the new institutional context.

vice-president of the commission. − mr president, your vote tomorrow will be a landmark on the road to effective gender equality in europe because the directive we are debating today will help to smash the glass ceiling that prevents qualified women from holding top posts in listed companies. i would like to thank the rapporteurs, evelyn regner and rodi kratsa-tsagaropoulou, as well as their shadow rapporteurs. they have done excellent work leading to a solid compromise. i have been closely following your discussions: the positive opinions from the imco, empl and econ committees and the joint report of the femm and juri committees, which has been adopted by such a large majority. now i would like to tell you that the europe-wide debate on this issue, which we launched together three years ago, has already led to improvements: women now make up 16.6% of directors compared to 11.8% in october 2010. this is a european average of course. without the debate which we launched three years, which was neutered by a call for self-regulation and which was followed by the report showing that self-regulation did not work and was in turn followed by the legislative proposal which led to the political and social discussion in all our member states about this proposal and then finally to the work led by the ep we would not now have had this five percentage point increase of female board members in listed companies in the last three years. but still, 16.6% is far from the balance we want and need to achieve. not only is this a waste of talent, it is also a missed opportunity to make better decisions leading to better financial results in the highest bodies of our listed companies. we all know that there are important economic arguments for this proposal. making use of female talent improves companies’ results. studies from many economic organisations or companies, such as ernst&young and crédit suisse – and dozens of others coming to the same results – show that companies with a higher share of women at top levels deliver stronger organisational and financial performance. more women in management positions provide a broader insight into economic behaviour, into consumers’ choices, leading to market share gains. diversity among board members boosts creativity and innovation and contributes to better performance because decisions are based on evaluating a wider range of options. your proposal will help to bring about change by obliging listed companies to take the procedural measures enabling them to attain the objective of the directive, a share of at least 40 per cent of the under-represented sex among non-executive directors. your proposal guarantees that only women who are very qualified will be given priority in selection for appointment and that will lead to better quality for men and women in selection for appointment. the proposal strikes the right balance between fast progress on the one hand and merit as a decisive criterion for holding board positions on the other hand. i welcome the fact that your report largely follows the spirit of the commission proposal and would like to support the approach you take on smes. whilst they remain exempted from obligations under the directive, member states would need to put in place policies to support and incentivise smes to significantly improve gender balance on their boards and management. i also welcome your ideas on detailing further the procedural obligations in order to achieve the 40%-objective as well as on reinforcing the reporting obligations. however, i am more cautious concerning the strengthening of sanctions as suggested in some of the amendments. under the commission proposal, member states’ responses must be effective, dissuasive and proportionate. to impose automatically one or more particular type of sanctions for any non-compliance could be disproportionate, but i leave this to the wisdom of the council and the parliament when it comes to the negotiation about the text. we know that the so-called glass ceiling prevents women from realising their full professional potential. it also prevents companies from using all talents available and becomes a risk for their competitiveness. this is a price that our society and our economy cannot afford to pay any more, most of all not in times of economic hardship. most of all not in times of looming demographic crisis. europe needs all talent on board, be it male or be it female. so let the adoption of this report be a signal to the council that progress on this dossier is urgently needed.
rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on the internal market and consumer protection. − mr president, first i would like to thank my colleagues for the incredibly hard work they have done on this report. as a rapporteur for the committee on the internal market and consumer protection (imco), i would like to stress that we very much welcome the commission’s proposal. we are a committee that, in the light of various statistics, facts and evidence, supported this proposal. we see the directive as a key instrument in achieving financial stability and long-term economic growth. in terms of the current economic environment, as numerous qualitative studies have demonstrated, there is a positive relationship between gender diversity at top-management level and a company’s financial performance and profitability. women have proved to be very valuable; better gender balance contributes to better business performance, improved competitiveness and economic gains. increased female representation on the boards of publicly listed companies in the union is not only related to the principle of gender equality and non-discrimination, but also to a positive impact on the organisational and financial performance of companies concerned. as a rapporteur in imco, i would like to stress that not utilising the potential of half of europe’s population for company board positions might slow down the development of our economy, the recovery of our financial structures and harnessing of the capacity of the available human capital.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should first like to congratulate the two co-rapporteurs, mrs kratsa-tsagaropoulou and mrs regner, as well as all the other committees, for the long work they have done on this text. we would have preferred not to have had to resort to legislative provisions, but at the rate things are going, on the basis of the goodwill of economic actors, we know it will take another 40 years to achieve some semblance of gender balance in senior positions in large companies. this is a matter of concrete measures to promote women's participation in economic decision-making. this is a pragmatic text proposed by the european parliament. firstly, we have been able to remain reasonable by keeping smes out of the scope of the directive. we are particularly concerned with the establishment of transparent and non-discriminatory appointment and election procedures rather than with the quota itself. we will thus allow women to break the glass ceiling without having to suffer the disadvantage of women's quotas. it should also be noted that by focusing the sanctions on the mandatory procedures rather than on the 40% figure for women, a more positive dynamic for gender equality is established. these two key elements help us to advance gender equality without penalising businesses. it's just as important! tomorrow we can say that the european parliament has done its part. it is now up to the member states to roll up their sleeves and put into practice the commitments they have made to gender equality in europe. i hope that the leading countries, which already have national provisions of this kind, will be able to convince their neighbours to take this step forward. in conclusion, let me remind you that promoting gender equality is not just a women's issue. in these times of crisis, who wants to do without the human capital that all these women represent, trapped by this glass ceiling? this will benefit all european citizens and the european economy. that is exactly what they expect of us - actions, not vows or promises!
on behalf of the alde group. mr president, it was a pleasure to work with both rapporteurs. i agree that we are now faced with the opportunity to make a historic breakthrough, our efforts will be justified. the european union is founded on the principle of dual legitimacy the legitimacy of decisions emanates both from the member states and from citizens. however, the protocol to the treaty of lisbon recognised that the seat of the institutions of the union is determined by common accord of the governments of the member states. this gives an advantage to the states over the citizens, thus breaking the principle of the union's dual legitimacy. and if the other institutions are shaped by national characteristics, the european parliament is directly elected by the citizens. this makes it a more specialised institution, with a function and a role different from those of national parliaments. today our institution is functioning in a complex situation, the parliament is equal to the co-legislating council, but its workload is many times greater, its polycentric structure makes our work considerably more difficult. it is this inconvenient and impractical situation that is provoking this debate, in which we are not just discussing the seat of one of the institutions, but are actually talking about how decisions are made in the union and what we want its legitimacy to be, especially in the light of the upcoming elections. the report of the committee on constitutional affairs, adopted by a large majority, clearly states that parliament should have the right to decide where and when to sit. that is the philosophy of the speakers, and i would like to congratulate them once again. the report is not about deciding whether brussels or strasbourg is the place. there is no call for neglecting one's role, no advance decision-making. the report calls for the european parliament to be given the right to organise itself, and such a cause is a just cause. it is well known that the requested change is complex and will require amendments to the treaties. but that's a technical issue. what european citizens need to hear is: do we want to have an effectively functioning union or are we willing to sacrifice pragmatism in the name of narrow national interests?
mr president, first of all, congratulations on the hard work of all colleagues and the european institutions. we wanted a fairer, greener and more flexible cap. some of our goals were achieved. we can congratulate the european parliament for its role in the process of defending the interests of european farmers. we also made good compromises: on direct payments, more flexibility between pillars, more production-related support. this is a good message for livestock farmers, vegetable growers and fruit growers. the most important thing is the young. the compulsory scheme for young farmers is a signal of our commitment to the future of european agriculture, because only through clear objectives, innovation and concrete actions can we keep young people in the sector. of course, there were also notes of disappointment. beekeepers, rose growers, tobacco growers could find a place in the first pillar, because it's about equity between sectors and jobs. but we must not be fatalistic. there is a rural development programme, there are voluntary schemes which allow national specificities to be adequately reflected. now i want to say this: the responsibility of the member states to make adequate decisions according to the needs of the sector is very great. they should identify measures that are effective in their implementation, in dialogue with farmers. this is the only way to achieve a more successful cap.
in writing. − colleagues, i supported the general regulation on the european structural and investment funds (esif), which creates a common strategic framework for all the funds with the aim of an integrated approach to their implementation, a focus on results, simplification of procedures and greater flexibility in spending. the esi funds remain the most important investment instruments in the new mff to reduce the burden on national budgets. i supported macroeconomic conditionality because financial discipline should not be weakened by the poor financial performance of certain member states and the fear of future financial corrections. the removal of such conditions is in favour of socialists who are inclined to policies of higher debt and deficit in the member states in order to implement populist measures with unsecured social costs. without clear rules and restrictive measures for non-compliance with macroeconomic conditionality, national governments will have no incentive to be disciplined, and those that are will find it difficult to overcome euroscepticism and explain to their citizens the reasons for their cuts and restrictions. the less developed economies, despite the temptation to increase debt and deficits, should achieve the catch-up effect of their economies, through european means, as a long-term investment to improve living conditions and quality of life. thank you, thank you!
in writing. − i voted for this report because it aims to simplify the complex administrative system for financing, managing and monitoring the cap, both for farmers and for the administrations themselves. the report focuses on new control and sanction systems, an early warning system and the reduction of control and risk associated with inspection procedures. it aims to reduce red tape and streamline the system of farm advisory boards. the cap monitoring and evaluation system and clearer rules on cross-compliance are taken into account. an important aspect of transparency in compliance is the principle of proportionality in the system of sanctions and checks. this is a new approach where action taken on infringements must be proportionate not only to the infringement committed but also to the degree of delay and the degree of responsibility of the farmer. at the same time, we have an early warning system in which beneficiaries are informed of the possibility of irregularities with the aim of not sanctioning but removing those irregularities. this will result in properly functioning control systems and a low error rate. that is why i voted for this report because it proposes clearer, simpler and more transparent rules for the financing, management and monitoring of the cap.
in writing. − i voted for this report because it is a step forward towards a model of rural development based on one of the main principles of the new cap: flexibility for member states and the possibility to adapt to their own specificities. in the national rural development programmes, the parties will have the possibility to identify those measures which will be most effective in their implementation. this is an expression of the solidarity of the european institutions with national realities, but it also represents a great responsibility for the member states, which must make appropriate decisions according to the needs of their specific sectors. we must not forget that much of the results will come from the way the rules laid down in the cap are implemented at national level. agroecological measures will be the focus of rural development programmes. it is important that they are successfully promoted among farmers in order to maximise the benefits of their application. the other basic principle of the cap, equity, is also enshrined by increasing the co-financing rates to 85% for the less developed and remote regions. that is why i voted for this report, because it contains measures which can be applied and targeted at the maximum number of farmers and regions.
in writing. - i voted for this report because it is a step forward towards a more effective common organisation of agricultural markets. the report improves the functioning of the food chain by facilitating cooperation between farmers, interbranch organisations and producer organisations. the report focuses on crisis management, public intervention and private storage, which are key instruments for regulating disturbances in agricultural markets. the scheme for aid to the most deprived, which is set out in a separate instrument, and the scheme for the supply of fruit at school shall continue. by doing so, we are sending key messages to those who need our attention the most - the most needy and the children. a mechanism to regulate vine planting until 2030 has been adopted. the current system of planting rights will be replaced by the system of planting authorisations, which is again a tool for exercising control in the sector. we have managed to take action against the industrialisation of production, the destabilisation of the market and a commitment to preserving the reputation of quality of eu wines. as regards the beekeeping sector, two additional specific support measures are added to the existing six in the national programmes: market surveillance and improvement of product quality in order to exploit the potential of the products on the market. this shows the willingness to scale up actions to improve the sector's situation. that is why i voted for this report, because it is a step in the right direction to protect european producers and european production.
in writing. - i voted for this report because it is a step forward towards a more flexible, fairer, green and young-oriented common agricultural policy. the report reflects most of the objectives of the european parliament, which in the negotiation process has taken the position of protecting european farmers. at the same time, this report allows member states greater flexibility to find effective solutions according to their own agricultural structures and agro-economic conditions. the greater flexibility between the pillars, the greater amount of coupled support, is a good message for livestock farmers, vegetable growers and fruit growers. another particularly important focus is on the young. we have sent a signal of our commitment to the future of european agriculture. only through clear objectives, innovation and concrete actions can we keep young people in the sector. that is our primary duty. that is why i voted for this report, because i think it provides a good basis for meeting the challenges, both at pan-european level and in each member state individually.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it is the necessary basis for a gradual and smooth transition to the new cap. as regards direct payments, it was important that some of the elements agreed as part of the overall cap reform could enter into force on 1 january 2014. these are the possibility for member states to transfer funds between the two pillars of the cap, the possibility to grant a redistributive payment to small producers, and the application of the new rules on transitional national aid in member states applying the single area payment scheme. the provision of an opportunity to increase coupled support and to exempt small farmers from financial discipline is a step in the right direction. in the context of rural development, it was important to reach a compromise that would allow the smooth continuation of the current rural development schemes in view of the transition between the existing and the new mff. that is why i voted for this report because it is the necessary link between the old and the new eu cap.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, i welcome the horizon 2020 rapporteurs, thanks to whom the new programme will develop science for the benefit of society and the competitiveness of the european economy. i am pleased that the emphasis is increasingly shifting not only to efficiency but also to the efficient use of funds. i welcome the commission's administrative simplification measures, which do not come at the expense of effective control and allow for more effective participation of young scientists and potential beneficiaries from central and eastern europe. however, the following three challenges remain on the agenda: first, the equalization of the salaries of scientists in order to achieve an open and attractive european labour market with flexible mobility, which is not only from the periphery to the centre, i.e. from the new to the old member states. secondly, access for scientists from the new member states to the network already established by the old member states. all potential boundaries between european scientists must be overcome in order to develop the full innovative potential of the european economy. third, more synergy and coherence of systems between horizon 2020 and the structural funds to achieve a 'multiplier effect', i.e. a higher return on investment for every euro invested, with tangible positive effects on the economy and citizens' daily lives. in conclusion, we must not allow horizon 2020 to become a one-way street from the periphery to the centre and a brain drain outside the european union.
madam president, horizon 2020 is both an achievement and a major challenge for the european union. smart, inclusive and sustainable growth can only be achieved by seriously mobilising and integrating the community's scientific, educational and industrial resources. this is particularly important in the context of insufficient funds. as we look at horizon 2020 as a package, we should also look at the challenges that will accompany its implementation: the real simplification of procedures to widen the scope and the boundaries of participation, especially by the new member states; to create conditions for the dissemination, application and multiplication of scientific products by creating research transfer exchanges; ensure equal understanding and legal protection for innovations, which will create a level playing field and accelerate their implementation; protect intellectual property; it also develops public-private partnerships, promotes national policies and legislation. it is particularly important to create conditions for the enhancement of competitiveness by small and medium-sized enterprises.
madam president, small and mediumsized enterprises in bulgaria face three main difficulties in relation to the european funds. the first is the question of influence peddling, because if your small firm has no connections somewhere at any level of government, it has absolutely no chance of getting its project approved and i urge you, commissioner, to do a thorough check and see how european projects are distributed and whether there are small businesses that are not connected to the government and how difficult it is for them to get funding. the second problem is the political-economic mafia that racketeers businesses in order to fill the party's black coffers. and the third problem is the racket of the banks, who blackmail small entrepreneurs, impose conditions and contractors on them, as they please, to such an extent that recently in my country a bank employee was murdered by a man who was racked, or at least so he claims, by his bank for carrying out a european project. that's what i wanted to tell you.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, the economic and financial crisis has affected the issues of employment, confidence, motivation and the choice of young people to stay in europe. developing entrepreneurial spirit and skills is one of the most sustainable ways to tackle youth unemployment. in the short term, this will create new jobs, and in the long term, it will bring about structural changes in the labour market, more flexibility and added value for the european union economy. the 'entrepreneurship 2020' plan should therefore be implemented targetedly among young people in three main directions: first, to change the perception of entrepreneurship by overcoming the culture of fear of failure. to this end, entrepreneurship training and practice and presentation skills should be integrated into the curricula and mentoring, career guidance and training schemes for unemployed young people should be established. second, each member state should take measures to reduce the administrative burden of starting a business. third, financial support should be provided at the initial stage of the business idea via tax breaks and/or access to lower-cost financing.
in writing. colleagues, the recovery of the european economy and its competitiveness requires support for small and medium-sized enterprises and entrepreneurship, which account for 60% of new jobs created. the enterprise competitiveness programme and the entrepreneurship action plan to 2020 aim to restore the entrepreneurial spirit in europe, which is seriously lagging behind the us and even emerging economies. the main challenges facing smes and entrepreneurship are access to capital, access to markets and over-bureaucracy. each of the cosme, erasmus+, horizon 2020 programmes includes lending mechanisms, but access to risk capital is particularly important, as setting up smes is a risky undertaking. unfortunately, there are no measures to activate the dormant capital held in deposits in banks. smes' access to markets, especially emerging markets, should be encouraged through active action by institutions and governments, which unfortunately is not well represented in the programmes to support entrepreneurs. overregulation and overburdened bureaucratic procedures are often insurmountable barriers to starting a small business. clear indicators are needed to reduce administrative burdens and to stop the constant changes in legislation. however, the adopted documents for the period 2014-2020 will inject oxygen into smes and start-up entrepreneurship.
ladies and gentlemen, every significant achievement in european integration is the fruit of long-term development and evolution in views and policies. the stage for more political and military integration has arrived, and the upcoming council is a definite test of political wisdom. in this connection, i would like to draw attention to three main points: first, ten years after the last eu security strategy, the council should launch a process of strategic re-thinking of the security environment with a new timeframe and political guidelines to reach a consensus among member states on the prioritisation of eu threats and objectives. the process should aim to generate support and a more open dialogue with european citizens. secondly, it is necessary to build full trust between member states in order to implement the pooling and sharing initiative. the creation of a 'european defence semester' would further streamline the use of national defence resources with a european dimension in mind. third, the crisis is an occasion to launch initiatives, not to postpone them. therefore, operational efficiency, defence capabilities and the european defence industry should be at the centre of the strategic debate.
mr. chairman, i'd like to say three things in this debate: first of all, i think it's clear to everyone that mistakes don't mean abuses. however, the errors should be brought to the attention of the taxpayers in the respective countries. they are not a waste of european money, they are a waste of national resources which must compensate for the mistakes made with european money, and here the report. but i think that periodically we should review the focus of the annual report. and this focus review should make the report useful to us. the change in methodology, including the different allocations of funds over the seven years of the financial period, makes it impossible to simply compare from year to year, and i see that a lot of people are misled by that. maybe there should be a
