indicated in your report that there is a problem with the stability of the european budget, more so in the payments area. indeed, the commitments made in the european budget are becoming increasingly difficult to meet. at the end of last year, there were 16 billion unpaid bills, and it's expected that by the end of this year, despite the extraordinary efforts of the parties, there will be 20 billion. it is obvious that the system of national budgeting of contributions to the european budget must also be changed, and i hope that the european court of auditors, in addition to identifying this problem, which is extremely serious, will be involved in changing the system, improving it, so that the european budget is stable and every euro that is committed is paid.
member of the commission. − madam president, i would like to thank parliament for debating roma inclusion strategies today. also, on behalf of my colleague commissioner reding, i am very proud to announce that the council adopted today, with unanimity, the first european legal text on roma inclusion – the recommendation on effective roma integration measures in the member states. this is a big achievement. it means that the need to act in a coordinated way has finally been widely recognised by all member states. so this is a success for the commission, which put this proposal on the table, but it is also a success for the european parliament, which has always been very supportive of an eu approach to roma inclusion. it is also a signal of hope for the roma population everywhere in europe. the european parliament now needs to give its consent to the text and i am sure that it will do that swiftly. this council recommendation is the first legal instrument within the european framework for national roma integration strategies. it strengthens the european framework by providing guidance to member states in the four key areas of education, employment, housing and health. it also calls on member states to take effective measures to combat anti-roma rhetoric and to better inform the roma about their rights and civic duties. member states are also encouraged to better support local and regional authorities, to facilitate transnational cooperation at all levels and to pay particular attention to the protection of children and women. in this context, i would like to thank ms járóka for taking up gender aspects in the national roma integration strategies. the commission considers gender equality to be a principle to be applied in all policy fields. the commission is well aware that roma women are particularly vulnerable. very often they are denied a fair chance on the labour market, equal access to education, healthcare and decent housing. some are victims of violence and exploitation, even within their own communities. this is why the situation of roma women is flagged up as being one of the cross-cutting issues in the council recommendation. the european commission has tried hard to ensure that european structural funds will be mobilised to boost national efforts to improve the social and economic situation of vulnerable groups, but member states are unfortunately not yet making the best possible use of them. negotiations on the partnership agreements and operational programmes on the use of european structural and investment funds for the period 2014 until 2020 are still ongoing. in this context, the commission is particularly vigilant and recommends that member states include roma integration as a priority. the commission has also set up a network of national roma contact points in all eu member states. this network is proving to be very useful for the exchange of best practices and peer review of the implementation of the national strategies. in addition, the commission is in permanent contact with the member states, roma civil society and all the stakeholders involved in the process of roma integration. national reform programmes under the europe 2020 strategy are also being scrutinised for coherence with national roma integration strategies. where appropriate, reference to roma integration is made in the country-specific recommendations. hence, in 2013, the commission issued country-specific recommendations on the roma to five member states. finally, the commission’s enlargement strategy highlights the precarious situation of many roma in the western balkans. the commission is helping the enlargement countries to support integration of roma through the instrument of pre-accession assistance. the commission is currently implementing or planning projects with a total value of more than eur 50 million that could benefit the roma communities. the commission will continue to monitor the progress and implementation of the national strategies and report on its findings. on 4 april 2014 we will hold a european roma summit in brussels. roma integration must remain a european priority. it is one of the great challenges of our time. it puts our spiritual strength and europe’s cohesion to the test, so we have to do our very best to ensure good results.
a question asked by raising a blue card. madam president, mrs clivetti, in relation to what you said about the patriarchal nature of roma society and that one of the main reasons young girls are not attending or are suspended from school is due to physical or mental violence mainly by their parents or their forced marriage, do you think that a suitable specific measure, as you said, would be, for example, an accelerated procedure for the withdrawal of parental rights when parents do not allow children to go to school, and i am speaking universally, not just in the roma case?
mr president, please note paragraph 65 of the report, because seven years ago i wrote an open letter to the rapporteur, mr jaroka, which provoked a very sharp and critical reaction. it is very interesting that what you called racism then, mrs. yaroka has now written, or at least its quintessence, in her report. thank you, madam speaker, for quoting me in this report. how could it be any different? isn't it when a human being goes from being dependent on one person to being dependent on another person for money that it's called "slavery"? can slavery be tolerated and can it be called a "minority tradition"? isn't it called pedophilia when you have sex with a minor? can pedophilia be tolerated and called a minority tradition? no, it can't, and i'm glad that mrs. yaroka wrote in her report, among many other things that were incorrect, that this can't be true. the solution to the problem is only one - just as all other peoples who suffered from similar barbaric traditions in the past have freed themselves from them, only the roma community can alone abandon this barbaric present in the future.
mr president, i call for an end to discriminatory treatment and speculation about a wave of migrants from bulgaria and romania, especially after the statement by british prime minister cameron that the free movement of people in the european union should be 'less free'. this approach creates negative stereotypes towards already legally resident bulgarians and romanians and shows political weakness to position themselves internally not through achievements, on the principle of fear and the negative campaign against european citizens. freedom of movement is a fundamental right. the uk cannot violate the eu treaties and benefit from the benefits of the european market. the data show that internal mobility within the european union has a positive effect on the economies of the member states, a large proportion of migrants are economically active, i.e. they migrate for work, and their impact on the social system is negligible, below 1%. bulgarians and romanians, who are not in the top 20 of the nationalities emigrating to the uk, are most often intelligent young people - an "economic bonus" that brings more taxes into its budget than the country pays in the form of social benefits.
in writing. madam president, the automotive industry is europe's largest industry and, as a whole, is of strategic importance to the european economy, providing almost 12 million direct and indirect jobs. given that this sector is currently facing serious problems related to stagnating demand in our markets and increasing competition from fast-growing external economies, we need to set the political direction for its sustainable development. moreover, considering factors such as the gradual change in the use of energy sources and the shift towards the digitisation of components and functions, a comprehensive rethink of our approach to mobility in the 21st century is needed. i believe that the resolution we are about to vote on reflects realistic objectives, the implementation of which will bring added value to the european automotive industry. the text sets out guidelines such as increasing competitiveness, investing in research and new technologies, and qualifying human resources, among others. i am glad that the resolution is sufficiently broad and seeks solutions to problems of a diverse nature such as restructuring, overcapacity, easier access for small and medium-sized enterprises to capital markets. ladies and gentlemen, that is why i am fully in favour of this resolution and i urge you to vote in favour of it.
the committee on women's rights and gender equality has also adopted a report on the situation in the european union. - mr president, the justice programme must serve us to defend the fundamental values of the european union, the first of which is equality between men and women. i would like to stress three aspects. first of all, it is about prevention, key word. the programme aims to prevent crime, particularly violence against women. there is a close link between justice and security, and everyone has the right to live in an area of justice and security. secondly, to facilitate access to justice. i know! but also better training of judicial and police staff to work with victims. it is our duty to build up trust in the judicial system and to make everyone aware of their rights and opportunities for justice. the third element is judicial cooperation. encouraging the exchange of good practice, improving the capacity to work together and establishing good practices in this area is essential, in particular to tackle the scourge of trafficking in human beings in europe, of which 60% of the victims are women. finally, the justice programme will enable european citizens to have access to a more efficient justice system by simplifying procedures and promoting their interests. it is up to us to see that it is implemented.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report because it is a text which takes the eu a step further towards creating a european area of justice. the proposed legislative measures are necessary and essential tools for the effective enforcement of legislation in the field of judicial cooperation in civil and criminal matters. this report follows up the new provisions of the lisbon treaty and the objectives set out in the stockholm programme. the justice programme is a follow-up to and a unification of the civil justice, criminal justice and drug prevention and information programmes. this confirms the eu's commitment to its citizens to facilitate access to justice and simplify procedures. finally, i am pleased that the report emphasizes equality between women and men, a fundamental european value to which i am committed.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report because it represents the eu's efforts and commitment to defend and promote human rights as enshrined in the treaty on the functioning of the eu and the eu charter of fundamental rights. the rights and citizenship programme follows on from and consolidates the fundamental rights and citizenship, daphne iii and the anti-discrimination and diversity and gender equality sections of the progress programme. the programme ensures better implementation of these programmes by simplifying their financing. violence against children, young people and women is a violation of fundamental rights. we must take firm and coordinated action to combat it. i therefore believe that the report rightly emphasizes the daphne programme. the success of this programme must serve as a blueprint for preventing and combating violence against children, young people and women and for promoting equality between women and men. finally, the programme has an added value in providing a better public awareness of their rights as european citizens.
mr president, i supported the report on the common fisheries policy because of the strong emphasis on regionalisation and 'regional participation in decision-making'. i am a representative of a maritime region of bulgaria and for the people of the bulgarian black sea coast this accent is extremely important. it is important that detailed technical rules in the different maritime regions are regulated in a decentralised manner and with the active participation of stakeholders working in the maritime areas. this mechanism should be translated into national strategies and plans. the bulgarian fisheries structures look forward to the next consultations with the commission on the establishment of the black sea advisory body. it will certainly play an important role in the development of the region, in reaching consensus, in providing a boost to blue growth based on black sea interaction. the black sea, as an eu border, is an important area and we expect a stimulus for development with the support of the european maritime and fisheries fund.
mr. chairman, of course i voted in favour of the mortgage credit directive. it was high time to start something that i campaigned on in my election campaign, namely regulation of the banking sector, which would now also apply to mortgages, because these mortgages, as we all know, relate to a fundamental, one might even say human right the right to own a home, to own a home and thus to a decent life. there is a huge amount of arbitrariness at the moment by banks that are seriously suspected of being in a cartel and imposing horrendous conditions on their customers. consumers have no legal means of objecting to this because they are presented with huge long contracts with very thin text, which have huge penalties that citizens cannot afford. they are also somewhat illegal and ultimately many hundreds of thousands of families in bulgaria, across the european union are in danger of losing their homes and therefore their decent way of life. that's why i voted for the directive to curb these bad practices.
mr president, i voted against the resolution adopted by the majority in the european parliament today on the report on sexual and reproductive health and rights, because the proposals in the estrella report were of great public interest and did not concern women only. sexual and reproductive health and rights should be ensured and protected for every living being at every stage of life. i regret that in the second decade of the 21st century the european parliament adopted a conservative resolution. we need to treat women's rights with respect, including sexual and reproductive health, the right to sexual education, the protection of young women and mothers, protection against sexual assault and violence, the right of women to plan their own lives, the right to privacy. to this right, the whole of society must be sympathetic. the estrella report has raised extremely important issues that cannot be delayed: educational programmes, measures to work with young underage mothers, future mothers to provide support to deal with the problems of early motherhood, measures to prevent forced early marriages that cause severe psychological and sexual harm to the young girls affected.
mr president, i voted in favour of the alternative resolution, which effectively rejected the estrella report. i want to tell you about a very interesting experience of my own. for the first time, at last, at the very end of this mandate, i felt like a member of parliament who is not only defending the national interests of his own country, although i am sitting in this group where we are united these members of parliament. i felt for the first time as a representative of european citizens, standing up for the common european values that are embedded in the healthy, family, traditional values of our continent, which have made our continent what it is in the field of tolerance. i am a husband and father myself, i take full responsibility for family planning for my family, i do not force my wife to take contraceptives and i fully respect her wishes regarding the future of our family. so i don't need a directive, and my child doesn't need a directive to teach him how to manage his sex life. i'm sure i can teach my child better than any government teacher who can put indoctrination in his head.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report because i believe it is essential to encourage and create an enabling environment for volunteering in europe. the report presents in a clear and comprehensive way the main elements and results of the initiative 'european year of volunteering' launched in 2011. it is an initiative that extends the voluntary sector and strengthens solidarity and the democratic and pluralistic values that are at the heart of our european society. although the current economic crisis has posed some difficulties in its implementation, the good results and success of the project are evident. this initiative can thus serve as a lesson and as an incentive for member states to continue to give strong and stable support to volunteering activities. i am pleased that the report mentions the positive impact of volunteering activities in the fight against gender inequalities. i strongly support this fight in accordance with the fundamental european values which reject any kind of discrimination. finally, the adoption of this report by the european parliament sends a strong message about the positive impact of volunteering activities.
in writing. - ladies and gentlemen, i have supported the report on volunteering and voluntary activity, which seeks to promote its development in europe, its dissemination, the promotion of the fundamental values of solidarity and accessible participation by citizens. i supported mr scuria's report because i believe that a common structured and coordinated european approach to volunteering is needed, based on its multicultural dimension in the member states and a thorough analysis by the european commission of national volunteering practices and traditions. the creation of an enabling environment for the development of volunteering is possible through legislative regulation in the member states and through the reduction of the administrative burden on organisations in the field. at present, three countries - bulgaria, slovakia and slovenia - lack a legal framework for volunteering, 12 have one, and the rest regulate the sector through other existing general laws. i welcome the timeliness of the report, as volunteering helps to overcome one of the biggest challenges facing europe - youth unemployment and the structural mismatch between demand and supply in the labour market. volunteering helps to increase the mobility, social cohesion and competitiveness of young people in the labour market. it increases their chances of finding a job through the transversal skills acquired through volunteering.
mr president, after today’s debate we have to adopt the resolution addressing the eastern neighbourhood policy in a strategic way based on realities. extreme reactions cannot contribute to our long-term objectives. after all, we have to be credible and stick to our position that, as far as russia is concerned, this is not a zero-sum game. yes, russia’s pressure is totally unacceptable. yes, we support wholeheartedly the legitimate protests of the pro-european majority in ukraine. young people there support political values, not nationalistic ones. we put our values in this fta, as well as the principle ‘more for more’. in the long term, this would prove beneficial for these countries. this is the right approach, but was it enough as a policy? i am afraid not. we in the new member states know well that these ftas also involve high economic and social costs in the short term. commissioner füle partly addressed this issue today. we need to enhance all our possible channels of communication with the partners. we have to find new ways of constructively engaging russia. this house should give further support to the euronest parliamentary assembly. what we need is both strategic patience and perseverance.

the rapporteur on the opinion of the committee on budgets. − the instrument for pre-accession assistance, which is to be voted on tomorrow, is not just an instrument for financing reforms in the enlargement countries. it is an investment in their future, which has the potential to lead to an improvement in their economic situation and to positive reforms in political and social life. it is a guarantee of the european union's commitment to the enlargement process, proof that it is backing up with clear and concrete actions its promises for a european future for the western balkan countries and turkey. yet irreversible and sustainable reforms cannot be achieved with financial means alone. what is needed is political will, persistence and consistency, a break with the old oligarchic dependencies, making accession to the european union a national priority. all these are steps that only the enlargement countries can take, and we in the european union can only guide and support them. the pre-accession assistance tool is the first step. i hope we'll soon witness the final one, the joining itself.

from memorial, and in our conversations we heard a firm call to continue serving as a huge beacon for freedom and democracy. i would like to extend my gratitude to ms lochbihler for the excellent idea of organising the event and mr donskis for his leadership in making it happen. we were all particularly pleased that the sakharov award this year went to the 16-year-old pakistani schoolgirl malala yousafzai. as european parliament president schultz said, malala is a ‘global icon in the fight for the right for education’. her words left an enduring mark on all of us. our shared objective must be to enable all girls and boys to go to school regardless of where they live. girls and boys around the world must live and grow without fear. this is why we are laying the groundwork for a worldwide campaign against one of the most abhorrent violations of human rights: violence against children. we aim to enhance prevention, and to fund more effectively child protection systems. we want to raise greater awareness about children’s rights, including their right to freedom from violence. malala also reminded us that there are nearly 800 million people who still lack basic literacy skills, and almost two thirds are women. inequality between men and women permeates all aspects of society. girls are more likely to be kept out of school, to be forced into marriage and to be subject to violence. this is unacceptable. leaving aside the moral principle, gender equality is also about democracy and economic development as well. we all know about the wasted talent and the lost wisdom when a society refuses to break with inequality. this is why we are particularly proud of the successful outcome of the 57th session of the un commission on the status of women, where the eu took a particularly active stance as concerns the elimination and prevention of all forms of violence against women and girls. we are now preparing for the next session, which will take place in march 2014 and we are keen to achieve strong agreed conclusions. there are estimates that every year some three million women and girls in the world face female genital mutilation. we are committed to a zero tolerance policy of this cruel practice that violates the rights of women and girls and harms them in many ways. the adoption in november, on the occasion of the international day for elimination of violence against women, of a strategy towards the elimination of female genital mutilation is a demonstration of our commitment, jointly with our international partners. we are also taking stock of the millennium development goals and recognising the need to step up joint efforts. looking forward, the eu is convinced that gender equality and women’s empowerment should have a strong place in the post-2015 framework. we are expressing this view in the on-going discussions on the sustainable development goals. the adoption of the eu strategic framework and the action plan on human rights and democracy in 2012 was a landmark shift. for the first time, thanks to this comprehensive and systematic document endorsed by all eu actors including parliament, the eu has delivered on its pledge to put human rights and democracy at the centre of its policy. our 2012 annual report fully reflects, for the first time, this renewed engagement, both in its structure and content. we are pleased by the encouragement you have expressed in your report. it means that we are on the right path. at the same time, we have taken careful note of your remarks, criticisms and suggestions on how to improve and make progress. in 2013, we have continued to develop our action for the promotion of human rights and we have upgraded our own internal procedures, we have set up a network of human rights focal points in the delegations and at headquarters we have set up a specific brussels-based cohom working group. we have mostly focused, as a matter of priority, on the implementation of the various activities of the action plan, which require collective efforts from eu institutions and member states alike. we have been working to deliver on our commitment to incorporate human rights in trade, development and other external eu policies, as your report strongly suggests. we have continued to push for implementation of the un guiding principles on business and human rights and significantly, the commission has published human rights guidance for three business sectors. we have a strong and principled policy on the abolition of the death penalty. we have continued to target retentionist countries and to advocate for the abolition of capital punishment, as well as for an introduction of a moratorium on executions as a first step towards abolition. in the past 18 months, at least 60 statements were issued and more than 30 démarches carried out, specifying worrying trends in executions, imposition of death sentences and breaches of moratoria. the eu attended the madrid world congress against the death penalty on 12-15 june, where stavros lambrinidis addressed the closing ceremony. we have pushed ahead with our human rights country strategies. we prepared 146 strategies from all regions which were discussed by cohom and relevant geographical working parties in 2013. we have continued to engage in a number of human rights dialogues and consultations with our partner countries, and we will strive, in line with your suggestions, mr kukan, to make them even more meaningful and effective in the future. and we have continued to prepare guidelines to guide and inform the eu’s policies in key human rights areas. in june the foreign affairs council adopted the guidelines to promote and protect the enjoyment of all human rights by lgbti persons. the guidelines target discriminatory laws and policies, promote equality and non-discrimination, aim at combating violence against lgbti persons and support and protect human rights defenders. on numerous occasions the vice-president/high representative issued public statements and our delegations carried out démarches. we fully share the worries of mr kukan and honourable members concerning the trend of increased violence against journalists and restrictions on media freedom, as the numerous statements by the vice-president/high representative this year sadly show. in this context, your report of june 2013 on the freedom of press and media in the world was a timely and substantial contribution. it feeds into our preparation of new eu guidelines on freedom of expression online and offline, which is ongoing, in consultation with you and civil society. our engagement at bilateral level was both intensive and extensive: the vice-president/high representative and the eu special representative have discussed human rights issues with eu strategic partners, such as china or russia. but they have also extensively travelled to countries in transition, particularly in such crucial areas as asean, the gulf, and north africa. in bahrain the call of the eu was heard for the release of those arrested in the context of peaceful political activities. and in egypt, the vice-president/high representative has repeatedly called for the full respect for fundamental human rights as an indispensable condition for a transition towards democracy and has offered concrete eu support for a democratic transition, including further assistance to civil society and in the socioeconomic sector to help the most vulnerable groups of egyptian society. our assistance in these areas will continue. countries in transition are a particular challenge. we share the analysis of mr kukanʼs report in this regard, and deeply appreciate this support for our efforts. as the high representative always says, it is not for europe, nor any other outsider, to offer countries in transition any ready-made solutions. democracy and freedom are built from the inside, not imported from abroad. what we want to do, what the eu is trying to do, is to help egypt, tunisia and other partners to build the kind of democracy that meets the aspirations of all: women, men, young and old. we can help but only they can do it. not just organising free and fair elections – it is about building the civil society and the institutions that guarantee the rights of everyone. it means freedom of expression, freedom of belief, and respect for all communities. this is also the case in myanmar where the eu organised the first task force outside of the neighbourhood area last month with the active participation and contribution of members of this house, led by vice-president isabelle durant. the vice-president/high representative, together with aung san suu kyi, took part in a specific civil society forum, with over 60 participants from all spectrums of civil society in the country. the forum gave the eu an opportunity to highlight the importance it places on engagement with civil society as a key component of the transition, and to discuss the key challenges facing the country, including human rights, reconciliation, minorities and the quest for sustainable peace. the eu shares parliament’s concern about the crackdown on civil society organisations in many corners around the world and is deploying tremendous efforts to address increased restrictions on freedom of association placed on civil society. let me assure you that we take very seriously mr kukanʼs invitation to work closely with civil society. as an example, the eeas and devco are working together towards the establishment of road maps for civil society. only last week, we held the annual eu-ngo forum. hundreds of human rights defenders, activists and policymakers gathered in brussels to discuss accountability, including the icc and transitional justice. the european instrument for democracy and human rights (eidhr) has continued to support extensively human rights defenders and civil society. in this context, let me praise the constructive trialogue of 19 november which has paved the way for an agreed text on the new eidhr regulation. we are pleased that the new eidhr foresees a strengthened and more comprehensive human rights defenders mechanism, and a new facility to support civil society actors in the most difficult countries where human rights and democracy are most at risk. we are engaged within the united nations to advance the universality of human rights. very fittingly, this year human rights day marks the 20th anniversary of the world conference on human rights in vienna and the creation of the office of the high commissioner for human rights. the vice-president/high representative publicly commended the high commissioner navi pillay on the tremendous work that her office and the rest of the un have conducted over the past 20 years in furthering and safeguarding human rights throughout the world. the vienna declaration and programme of action stated the universality, indivisibility and interdependence of all human rights and led to historic advances in their promotion and protection. the united nations remains a vital forum for the advancement and protection of human rights, and the eu remains active and engaged in geneva, new york, and worldwide. in 2013 the eu has advanced its agenda through the un, as endorsed by the foreign affairs council conclusions of 18 february. the 2013 human rights council in geneva saw the eu successful in all its objectives, including by expanding the mandates of the special rapporteurs for myanmar, the dprk, freedom of religion or belief, belarus and iran, as well as securing the mandate for the commission of inquiry on syria. in new york, at the un general assembly third committee, eu resolutions on myanmar and the dprk passed without a vote, enjoying broad, cross-regional support. resolutions on iran and syria were also passed, again with strong eu and cross-regional backing. i also want to join your report in welcoming the establishment of the european endowment for democracy (eed). the first funding decisions have been made, to democracy activists in both the eastern and southern neighbourhoods. in closing, after taking stock of your work this year, allow me to express some satisfaction at our extensive common achievements. and once again thank you to mr kukan, to all the honourable members, and to this parliament for all your support in our common endeavour.
the committee on women's rights and gender equality has also adopted a report on the situation in the european union. - mr president, i congratulate the rapporteur, mr kukan, on his excellent work. the report has taken account of the opinion of the femm committee, and i thank it. first of all, i think we should welcome the firmness of the very first paragraph of the report. human rights are at the heart of the union's relations with all third countries, including strategic partners. this is where the union's external action differs from other diplomacy, and this reality must be given visibility. in this capacity, the eu special representative for human rights plays a key role and i would have liked him to be present for this debate. secondly, the union's human rights policy must continue to focus on the fight against impunity, including sexual violence, especially when it is used as a weapon of war. we know that this is happening in many parts of the world right now. finally, as regards armed conflict, i would stress the efforts that must be made to protect children in armed conflict and, as far as possible, to enable them to continue to go to school, and to keep them away from armed groups. these are the points which add value to european human rights policy and i am glad that the report reinforces them.
in writing. at a time when the eu is at a strategic crossroads, choosing the right direction for the csdp is crucial to its military capabilities. europe needs to develop its full range of capabilities if it is to remain relevant to its strategic interests and respond in a timely manner to new geostrategic challenges. the decisions of the next council, including on industry and technology, will therefore have a long-term impact in the future. the eu's defence industry and technology is still fragmented and poorly competitive in the global arms market. at the same time, european defence industries are not only important for security, by providing the armed forces with military equipment and materiel, but they are also a source of new jobs, innovation and economic growth. the eu's industrial base should be competitive and innovative. defence research and innovation is key to enhancing the competitiveness of the european defence industry. a potential solution to this is to promote the development of dual-use technologies through the new research and innovation programme horizon 2020. cooperation and non-duplication with nato will multiply the effect. member states should also deepen their cooperation in military technological development on a bilateral and multilateral basis.
in writing. i voted in favour of the brok report because it enables the common rules and procedures for implementing the instruments for external action to be set out in a coherent, comprehensive manner and in accordance with the values and objectives of the union's external action. first of all, the report which sets out the framework for the implementation of the six financial instruments of foreign policy has the merit of clearly recalling the values which led to the construction of the european union and which must be reflected in external action: democracy, respect for the rule of law, the universality of human rights, fundamental freedoms. the report also takes into account issues such as better predictability of aid and ownership of programmes by beneficiaries. the report also stresses the need to make the union's external action more visible through these six instruments, possibly through a communication and information strategy for the citizens of the beneficiary third countries. finally, the involvement of the european parliament in the preparation and programming of projects will make it possible to manage the instruments of external action more democratically, in line with the values promoted.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report on the proposal for a regulation which creates a new complementary partnership instrument for cooperation with third countries. this instrument takes account of the reality of a multipolar world where new emerging powers, with which we can no longer organise our relations in the logic of development aid, become strategic partners in a competitive environment. this instrument should make it possible to develop the european union's strategic relations with these new partners and, above all, to better project and defend european values, interests and models of society in the various global forums and international organisations. in defending its strategic interests, the eu must thus protect its values and specificities. this new instrument should help to make the eu an effective international player where member states can no longer weigh in on the balance alone. however, it will be necessary to ensure, in particular through the application of the regulation establishing common rules and procedures for the implementation of the instruments for the union's external action, that this partnership instrument is consistent with those for development.
in writing. − i voted for this report because it is a step forward in preserving the stability of milk production in mountain, less-favoured and outermost regions after the abolition of quotas in 2015. milk production is often the only source of income in these parts of europe. it is of particular importance for their economy, social stability, environmental quality and land use. therefore, a stable future for the sector must be ensured, depopulation of rural areas and negative economic and environmental consequences avoided. to this end, it is necessary to preserve livestock in these regions. it is not only an economic sector and a livelihood for their inhabitants, but is a defining element of their traditional culture and social structures, deeply linked to the daily life and traditions of the local population. attention should be paid to the condition of permanent grassland and pastures and to small farms in these regions. the important role of the coupled payments under the first pillar and the support measures under the rural development programmes for these areas should be underlined. that is why i voted for this report, because it is a step forward towards one of the objectives of the new cap - the maintenance of productive agriculture in the mountainous, disadvantaged and remote regions of europe.
member of the commission. − mr president, honourable members, the proposal on payment accounts that we are discussing today, for which i share responsibility with commissioner barnier, is of great importance. it will open up the single market for financial services for both consumers and businesses, thereby increasing competition, allowing consumers greater choice and driving down prices. it will also allow for better integration of all eu citizens in the economy by improving their access to financial services. as you know, all three institutions are working very hard to conclude this important file within the current mandate. so let me first thank parliament for its hard work and especially mr klute – the rapporteur for the committee on economic and monetary affairs (econ) – and the shadow rapporteurs for their determination, expertise and personal commitment to advancing this key file. you may recall that this proposal was one of the priority actions of the single market act ii and that parliament itself produced an own-initiative report with recommendations to the commission on access to basic banking services last year, again led by mr klute. you already know what this proposal aims to achieve. let me address three key elements which have been subject to particularly intensive discussions over the last couple of months, including in this parliament. first, on the transparency and comparability of payment account fees: it is essential that fee information provided to consumers before they open a payment account is both well-targeted and relevant, so that consumers find the information useful and easy to understand. long lists of fees would not help consumers understand; neither would they allow effective comparison with other offers. second: we are convinced that a reliable switching service across the union will benefit both consumers and providers. it is an important tool for increasing consumer mobility in the eu and will bolster the single market for financial services. we are ready to consider options that would help us reach this result within a reasonable time. third, it is imperative that consumers are able to freely, and without restriction, choose payment accounts most suited to their needs, wherever the provider is located in the european union. the principle of having a single market would be undermined if limitations were placed on consumersʼ ability to purchase goods and services cross-border. i must add that the commission is fully aware that all eu consumers must follow certain requirements, including identity checks, before they are able to open a payment account. the proposed directive does not derogate from full compliance with the anti-money laundering provisions set in the anti-money laundering directive. however, beyond these basics, there should not be any other criteria to be met. easier access to financial services will enhance customer mobility in the european union and will foster competition between providers, encouraging them to offer better and cheaper products and services. i will now be very interested to hear your views on these as well as other issues on which you think the commission proposal could be improved. i will carefully analyse all your comments and suggestions. i count on the honourable members to give a strong mandate to the rapporteur mr klute which will pave the way for successful negotiations, so that together we can deliver for citizens by concluding this important file within the current parliamentary term.
the rapporteur on the opinion of the committee on legal affairs. - mr president, unlike many less useful policies, consumer protection is one of the union's good things. in particular, despite my very particular opinion, i am in favour of subsidiarity, since in the banking sector in particular the bulgarian authorities are too weak to limit the arbitrariness of this sector over consumers. this directive, which we are going to vote on tomorrow, is a very important step forward in supporting consumers, because they would be hard pressed to defend themselves against the arbitrariness of such powerful institutions as banks. let every european citizen know that from tomorrow he will have the opportunity, the right to his own bank account, which will not burden him, will not burden his budget with absolutely unnecessary expenses for bank charges. because bank fees exist so that the bank can cover its real administrative costs. many banks, however, abuse the system by charging higher fees and turning them into a source of profit. and i congratulate the commission that it is now limiting this abuse by the banking sector in its proposal. citizens should also know that they will have an easy and accessible system to compare fees and make a much easier and much more accurate informed choice. i think that the compromises that have been discussed and the participation of all parties in this good dialogue that has taken shape during the work on the adoption of this directive have actually cleared out all the contentious elements of it and with our joint work in the legal affairs committee, the internal market committee and the leading economic affairs committee, we have indeed achieved a very important, solid result that is in the interest of every single citizen and in this way the european parliament has proved that it is close to the citizens of europe.
the directive under discussion, commissioner, demonstrates the european union's consistency in its efforts to manage and protect europe's water resources and coastal zones in an integrated manner. the expansion of maritime activities in coastal areas, the intensification of the exploitation of water resources and the intensification of industrialisation pose a number of challenges for the protection of the environment and the development of marine areas. we are seeing landslides and the generation of huge amounts of waste, and in fact a large proportion of the european union's population lives in coastal areas and we need to ensure a high standard and a clean environment for everyone. the report is balanced - congratulations, mrs meissner - and preserves the rights of the nation states in the regulation of coastal areas. i welcome the creation of an integrated electronic platform through which processes related to the regulation of marine areas will be monitored, as well as the inclusion of natura 2000 sites in the scope of the directive. the basis for maritime spatial planning should be to open up urbanised areas to the sea and to eliminate the trend towards the creation of coastal zones with restricted or paid access. water and coastal resources should be treated as a european common good and their planning processes should lead to their full use in the interests of people.
mr president, tackling the loss of biodiversity is of vital importance as we are well aware that it is closely linked to human wellbeing. our actions are needed not so much for the sake of achieving our 2020 climate objectives or our international commitments under the aichi biodiversity targets and the strategic plan for biodiversity: first and foremost they are needed to protect nature for future generations. one way to achieve this is by creating green infrastructure which successfully integrates nature into society. this means that ecological elements should be integrated at the very early stages of planning and developing infrastructure in urban, rural and coastal areas. for this purpose, green requirements should be integrated in rural infrastructure projects co-funded by the european union. besides the ecological and public health benefits, such an innovative approach will contribute to the creation of sustainable green jobs. i would also like to stress the need for our national and regional authorities to make good use of all available eu funding opportunities to support understanding and promote more green infrastructure initiatives. greening our cities should become a standard. finally, i would also like to highlight the need to promote research, innovation, capacity building and awareness-raising in this field. it will guarantee that the benefits and potential of developing green infrastructure are well known in society and the private sector.
